Calyx · new comparison study · no account deployment

Top 5 with Nasdaq DI OFF
versus Prop Ready with DI ON

$1,000.00 initial cash for each replay · 2026-08-17T00:00:00+00:00 → 2026-10-17T00:00:00+00:00 (end exclusive)

Top 5 uses its current client default: Nasdaq DI agreement OFF. Prop Ready retains Nasdaq and USDJPY DI agreement ON. Each portfolio keeps its own strategy rules, stops, exits and membership; this is not a clean isolated DI-only experiment.

This study uses fresh two-month Top 5 source tests and the frozen Prop Ready source tests—not the old Top 5 September 3-/6-month chart archive. That client archive remains explicitly historical DI-ON/skip-min evidence. Only the local Top 5 client default was changed; Prop Ready, website and live accounts were not changed. Historical tester mode permits research dates outside the client licence window by its existing design; the delivery licence was not renewed, extended or disabled.

Source feed is not the connected demo. Native source trades use isolated Exness-MT5Trial16 Raw-like pricing. Lot/margin snapshots target your Exness-MT5Trial15 USD demo, which is Pro-like and has different quotes/spreads. Source-account costs are not exact current-demo execution. Extra-cost sensitivity adds current snapshot spread at each side plus doubled negative swap, retaining source commissions—not exact historical Pro repricing.
5% risk is not supported as prop-safe by this comparison.

The as-packaged view compares what the launchers actually request, including their different safety policies. It is not equal risk. The common-policy view gives both portfolios the same research-only sizing and entry guards; it is not a change to either delivered package.

Top 5 delivered policy: fixed $50 ended at $1,038.17 cash (+3.82%), but suffered 54.54% sampled peak-to-trough equity DD. A small cash gain does not compensate for that drawdown. Its 5%-balance version returned -4.93% cash with 48.47% sampled DD. Extra-cost sensitivity changes fixed-$50 cash return to -6.70%. 2 ending position(s) remain open: their floating profit is not banked.

The selected amount is not a hard risk cap: the delivered minimum-lot override was used on 14 entries in fixed-$50 mode and 17 in 5%-balance mode. The largest fixed-$50 initial stop risk was $131.00 against a $50.00 budget (12.63% of entry equity), before gaps and financing.

Prop Ready’s quiet result is a cap, not a 5% test: -0.46% cash on 14 closed positions; both requested inputs still meet the $5 reference cap. It must not be called a better equal-risk 5% result.

Source-cost snapshot under the common research policy:

  • Fixed $50 — Top 5 · Nasdaq DI OFF: -22.13% cash / -22.13% sampled equity, PF 0.59, 22 closed; Prop Ready · Nasdaq/JPY DI ON: -17.14% cash / -17.14% sampled equity, PF 0.60, 18 closed.
  • 5% current equity — Top 5 · Nasdaq DI OFF: -18.31% cash / -18.31% sampled equity, PF 0.58, 20 closed; Prop Ready · Nasdaq/JPY DI ON: -18.19% cash / -18.19% sampled equity, PF 0.58, 21 closed.

All eight common-policy cases lost cash. The common guards are hypothetical 10%-daily / 20%-initial entry rules, not an FTMO account model. No 60–70% pass-rate result is computed or implied here.

Do not choose by the biggest sampled-equity return: open Nasdaq P&L may be unbanked, minimum-lot overrides can exceed selected risk, and guard thresholds can change which later trades are admitted. Two exposed months do not establish a best live portfolio or safe 5% risk.

Independent arithmetic/state audit: PASS · 827,481 checks against these exact frozen results. This verifies the replay, not a trading edge.

A real stop gap broke the research loss floor.

Top 5 common-policy fixed-$50 Nasdaq trade 36 crossed the weekend: its planned stop risk was $49.16 but the realized net loss was −$91.80. The Sunday reopening filled beyond the original stop, with weekend swap added. Ending cash $778.69 fell below the $800 research floor. This was a genuine source stop fill, not synthetic final liquidation or a sizing bug. An entry guard cannot guarantee an exit price or cap drawdown.

Priority follows the frozen rosters. Standalone signals are replayed chronologically against each portfolio’s cash, sizing and guard state. This is not a simultaneous native shared-account backtest, nor a subtraction of old portfolio P&L rows.

Read the two policy views correctly

  • As packaged · Top 5: fixed USD or percentage of current balance, floor lots then override up to broker minimum when needed. That override can exceed selected risk. No portfolio daily-loss/open-risk cap is invented. Estimated free margin is checked; actual broker liquidation is not reconstructed.
  • As packaged · Prop Ready: requested $50 or 5% of sampled equity still meets original source ceilings, the $5 reference cap on $1,000, strict minimum-lot skip, and existing shared/daily/reserve guards. Its quiet result is not comparable to uncapped Top 5 risk.
  • Common research · both: actual fixed $50 or 5% of current sampled equity; strict down-round/skip; 10% Prague day-anchor loss pause and 20% initial-capital new-entry halt. Projected/reserved equity must remain above $800; retain 125% original risk, known negative swap and $0.50/position. Aggregate/symbol limits 2×/1× current budget, 80% margin capacity, seven entries/three losses per day and a three-second wait.

Common-policy changes are hypothetical and not installed. This is not an FTMO challenge or funded-account pass model. Neither stop/guard policy guarantees realized daily losses or peak-to-trough DD; existing positions are not forcibly liquidated. A 20% loss needs a 25% recovery.

All case details · EAs, cash dates, trades and guards

Closed-position PF/win rate/streaks exclude open positions. Cash includes paid entry charges; sampled equity adds unbanked floating marks. Daily/monthly cash dates use Europe/Prague; UTC window boundaries create partial days. Relative monthly return is unavailable if opening cash is zero/negative. Sharpe uses all 61 UTC daily closed-cash returns and √365 when valid. Frequency is closed positions per weekday/calendar day, not guaranteed new signals per day.

Past-only five-minute owned-position samples are carried forward with timestamps; no future interpolation. Drawdowns are sampled estimates, not tick-certified portfolio extremes. Plot reduction is presentation-only. Native boundary-forced closes remain in original cash-reconciliation evidence but are censored in this replay, not booked as natural exits. Explicit final/export-only snapshots are excluded from owned-position floating marks. An admitted boundary-censored position therefore remains open, with paid entry charges in cash and its last valid floating mark unbanked. Export-only audit corrections do not change the strategy rules or make these exposed dates out of sample. Missing open-swap samples and stale-mark ages are disclosed in each case.

Initial risk and margins prefer source entry-time OrderCalcProfit/OrderCalcMargin receipts. Linear lot resizing cannot recreate changed liquidity tiers, broker stopout, current-stop timelines or new signals caused by rejected trades. Cost/rounding changes can produce different admitted trade paths; more-cost cases are not necessarily paired-trade deductions.

These are already-exposed recent dates, not untouched validation. No optimization, Monte Carlo, prop pass probability or low blow-up estimate is claimed. Investor.gov · general leverage risks.
